- Offer expedited shipping upgrades, such as 24-hour delivery for an extra $50, only when the supplier can actually deliver at that speed. Confirm the supplier's capacity before offering it (Source A, 2018, older). [L1109.043]

### Extended service and protection offers

- An extended service guarantee bought from a supplier at about $20 per year can be resold to the customer for about $100 per year. This works only with a supplier that agrees to the deal, and it is rarely done (Source A, 2020, older). [L0468.093]
- A guaranteed free return gives shoppers confidence to add an item they are unsure about, which raises AOV. The effect is strongest when the shopper knows a return costs nothing. [L0955.022]
- Checkout Plus case: a US apparel brand with about $12 million in annual revenue ran a 50/50 A/B test over two weeks. Half of its shoppers saw the Checkout Plus option in the cart and half had a normal cart and checkout. The Checkout Plus group showed a 2.7% increase in checkout conversion, a 2.4% decrease in cart abandonment and a 4% increase in AOV. These are relative changes, not percentage points. [L0955.020]
- Package protection in the cart: the speaker reported that conversion rate was not adjusted in 99% of cases, while about 70% of checkouts that offered package protection accepted it. Treat these vendor-reported figures as indicative. [L0955.021]
- Formula for cart offers: state a clear benefit in a few words and attach a small fixed price, so the shopper sees the trade-off at a glance. It works because it turns a vague future cost into a visible, cheap choice now.
  - Examples: "Add free returns for $3.50 before you check out" and "Pay $2.99 now to skip the $12 return fee later". [L0955.025]

### Post-purchase funnels: structure and discounts

Post-purchase funnel stages and discounts (example structure, Source D 2026):

| Stage | Offer | Discount | If accepted | If declined |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Upsell 1 (Dynamic) | Same product as bought, second unit (buy two) | 25% | Go to Upsell 2 | Go to Downsell |
| Upsell 2 (AI Generated) | Recommended product, bundle of three units (buy three) | 35% | Customer adds the bundle; funnel continues | Go to Downsell |
| Downsell (AI Generated) | Upsell product as the final best offer | 40% | Order gets the added item | Customer goes to the Thank You or Order Status page |

[L1141.003]

- The first post-purchase offer on a different item is typically set at 30-40% off so it reads as an add-on deal. The discounted price must also make sense next to the product page price for the same total quantity. [L1141.004]
- Source C uses a different ladder: a single-item buyer gets the same product at 15% off as the first upsell, and if declined, the same product at 30% off as a downsell. [L1412.031]
- Post-purchase funnels can have several levels: if the buyer accepts an offer such as a product at 30% off, the next offer can be a different item at 50% off. Typically two to three post-purchase offers are shown in sequence. [L1411.023]
- The thank-you page after an upsell can carry aggressive offers, such as 30 to 60% off, alongside the order, address and payment details. The buyer is already in purchase mode, so these deals convert well. [L1411.024]
- Showing the same price again after purchase is a weak post-purchase offer and is unlikely to convert. The offer needs a visible discount that is clearly better than the original price. [L1411.021]
- Offering the same item at a discount tends to convert best, even for expensive or heavy products. Test this before assuming the buyer will not repurchase the same item. [L1412.032]
- The final downsell line concedes that this is the best deal available. It is shown with the highest discount and a countdown, so the last offer feels like a fair final price instead of pressure.
  - Examples of the pattern: "Okay, this is our final price on the second copy, and it is the lowest we can offer." and "Last chance: this is the best deal we can give you on this add-on." [L1141.032]

### Funnel triggers, branching and timing

- Funnel triggers in Source D' setup: Product variant (is any), Advanced trigger settings, and trigger points Product Page Upsell (customer on product page), Shopping Cart (checkout button clicked), Page with Product Buy Box (Add To Cart button clicked) and Cart Drawer Upsell. Cart Drawer Upsell can only be placed on the OCU Slide Cart Drawer, and Product Page Upsell does not support the Product Variant trigger. [L1141.017]
- A Catch-All Funnel triggers when no other funnel conditions match, so every customer sees pre- and post-purchase offers. Funnels have a Priority setting that decides which funnel wins when several match. [L1141.018]
- Post-purchase upsell offers appear on the Thank You page or the Order Status page after checkout. The funnel can also show the offer on a percentage of traffic volume. [L1141.019]
- Trigger conditions for an upsell funnel can be set under product purchase data, choosing a specific product or the entire order. An order details condition such as unit or items total item count equals 1 targets single-item buyers. [L1412.030]
- Branch the post-purchase upsell by the number of units ordered. A single-unit buyer gets the same product at a discount, while a buyer of two or more units gets a different companion product. [L1412.056]
- The trigger can be a specific product in the order. Each trigger product can then get its own first upsell, so a buyer of one product sees a related offer and not a generic one. [L1412.057]
- In the post-purchase upsell app, set a countdown timer on the upsell page, for example 10 minutes, so the offer expires quickly. The timer is a field in the offer settings and works alongside the discount and button text. [L1412.058]
- Single-item buyer path: offer the same product at 15% off as the first upsell. If accepted, offer a different product from the same niche as a cross-sell. If declined, offer the same product at 30% off as a downsell. [L1412.031]
- Accept and decline buttons: the post-purchase offer should show a prominent Pay now button and a small Decline offer option. The decline option should be a small, low-visibility text link, while the accept button stays prominent. [L1412.005, L1410.020]
- Mandatory upsell screens that the customer must decline before completing an order draw complaints. Keep add-ons optional and clearly labelled. [L1164.021]

### Thank-you page content

- The thank-you page can show product recommendations, a share button, a collection section and more offers. These elements can be edited in the app's thank-you page settings, which are on by default. [L1412.037]
- The default thank-you page in ReconvertUpsell already includes product recommendations and a share button. The products and collections shown can be changed in the app's thank-you page settings. [L1410.042]
- A birthday form on the post-purchase page collects customers' birthdays, so a small gift such as an Amazon or store gift card can be sent. This is an optional way to build loyalty. [L1412.038]
- A ReconvertUpsell thank-you page can ask the buyer for a birthday, and the seller can then send a gift card as a birthday gift. [L1410.041]

### Testing funnels and offers

- Test multiple offer variations and multiple products, because the best complementary item is not known in advance. Compare the acceptance rate of each offer before choosing the one to keep. [L1411.025]
- Each offer in a Rebuy flow can run as an A/B test. The flow view lists offers with their own conversion, revenue and revenue per user, which can be compared to choose the winning version. [L1413.012]
- Create a variant by copying an existing upsell or downsell widget. In the example initial offer test, traffic is split 25% to one variant and 75% to the other. [L1413.020]
- In the example merchant's dashboard, the initial offer converts at about 16.20%, and the downsell row shows about 15.42% conversion. These figures are read from a screenshot and should be checked against the live report before being quoted. [L1413.021]
- Bundle and cart test procedure (Source C):
  1. Run a 7-day test with the bundle strategy.
  2. Then run a parallel 3-7 day test of Monster Card and other tools (ReConvert post-purchase, etc.).
  3. Compare final AOV to determine which approach works best. [L0298.059]
- Post-purchase upsells are worth setting up even for small stores, and a store should compare offers by acceptance rate rather than by assumption. [L1410.040, L1411.025]

### AOV effects

- Upsells and cross-sells raise average order value and profit margin when placed at the right point in the purchase path. [L0434.017, L0435.017]
- Average order value can be raised through add-ons such as an expedited shipping upgrade, an extended warranty, or a related physical product. [L0970.100]
- Checkout Plus raised AOV by 4% (relative) in the reported case, alongside a 2.7% relative increase in checkout conversion. [L0955.020]
- Bundles with strong discounts raise AOV significantly even for simple products. [L0298.034]
- Pre-selected low-priced add-ons: about 60% of orders may accept an extra $3 to $5 this way. [L1410.014]
- Cart reminder and recommendation changes produce conversion lifts (+9.2% reminder box, +4.5% cart recommendations) that also support basket size. [L0197.011, L0197.017]
- Vendor and speaker figures in this section (Checkout Plus, package protection, pre-selected add-on acceptance, Oodie results) are indicative and should be verified against your own store data before they are used as targets. [L0955.021, L1410.014, L0197.004]

#### Further details

- Add the product to the cart on the main product page before the customer initiates checkout. For proven concepts, this pre-warming of the cart works better than sending the customer straight to checkout. [L0560.016]

- Post-purchase automation email sequences are the strongest way to raise average order value after the sale. They work alongside cross-sells, upsells, faster shipping options and extended warranties. [L1109.041]

## Email and phone capture: pop-ups, opt-ins and lead magnets

### Why capture contact details first

- A valuable free resource that matches what the visitor really needs turns cold and warm audiences into prospects by capturing name and email. [L0240.002]
- Research what the target audience genuinely wants, then build a free resource (eBook or report) that addresses those needs. Include company information and relevant offers alongside the value-driven content to introduce the business. [L0240.003]
- Older benchmark (2020): a new store with 100-150 qualified visitors per day performs acceptably. Five email subscribers from that traffic is below target and shows that email capture needs optimization. [L0245.041]
- Collect customer emails for follow-up, including abandoned cart recovery and bounce-back campaigns. Email follow-up significantly affects conversions. [L0249.032] (older, 2022)
- A sign-up pop-up that offers a small discount for an email address can raise conversion and grow the list at the same time. Run it from the start of the store. [L0476.003]
- Ask every visitor for an email address in exchange for something of value, such as a free report or video. Visitors unwilling to give an email address are unlikely to pay you later. Example: 1,000 daily visitors at a 30% opt-in rate produce about 300 interested leads. [L0523.060]
- Few shoppers want another newsletter, but many will hand over an email address for something free. An opt-in bribe makes the trade worth it. [L0757.011]
- A new brand selling only on pre-order struggles to convert because it lacks trust and authenticity. Collecting sign-ups or notify-me requests is a softer way to capture demand. [L1016.053]
- Capture visitors' contact details and set up exit-intent overlays so visitors who leave can be remarketed to. [L1002.033] (older, 2019)
- An exit-intent overlay captures visitor emails at the moment they leave and is a core tool for building the list early on. [L0963.059] (older, 2022)
- Even with a small ad budget, use an exit-intent overlay to collect emails from visitors who are leaving. Captured emails feed the abandoned cart and promotion emails. [L0468.088] (older, 2020)
- Combine a notification bar, an exit-intent overlay and email capture with an automated follow-up to turn first-time visitors into buyers sooner. Also set up abandoned cart emails. [L1088.059] (older, 2020)
- Blog posts are a good place to collect email leads. Adding an email signup section to the blog posts template helps build the list. [L1265.005]
- For paid traffic, set the lead ad destination to a squeeze page that offers a free gift, a discount coupon or a popular product in exchange for an email opt-in. Sell offers to opted-in people over time by email. Enter the destination URL in the website URL field of the ad. [L0641.006]

### Where opt-ins go on the site

- Every content page should carry a content-based opt-in: blog posts, the blog index, the home page and collection pages. Use a sidebar opt-in, an inline opt-in below the content on blog posts and collection pages, and an exit-intent pop-up on all pages except product pages. [L0571.007] (older, 2021-22)
- Offer opt-ins on every page of the site, not only in the footer or on the home page. Use different opt-ins for different parts of the site so visitors at different funnel stages get relevant offers and the message is not repetitive. [L0571.024] (older, 2021-22)
- Footer-only opt-ins are seen by very few visitors, since only about 20 percent of visitors scroll to the bottom of a page. Scroll-mapping software can show this on most sites. [L0571.025] (older, 2021-22)
- Running only one opt-in offer across the whole site is a common error. Visitors arrive at different stages of the buying journey, so one offer cannot match all of them. If an opt-in rarely gets sign-ups, it is usually the first thing to fix. [L0571.002] (older, 2021-22)
- Blog layout: make the top third of each blog post a squeeze-style opt-in section where the only available action is entering an email address. Visitors from search, social or guest content see it first. This turns the blog into a conversion tool rather than only a content page. [L0523.026]
- Place an inline opt-in in the middle of a blog post. An opt-in for a related downloadable guide on the same topic has a high opt-in rate, because the reader is already interested in that subject. [L0571.009] (older, 2021-22)
- Place a related content opt-in below the author information at the end of the article. Readers who reach the bottom have just finished the article and are most interested in the topic. [L0571.041] (older, 2021-22)
- In the Manhattan theme, the email signup section on blog posts can be given an accent color to make it stand out. The change is saved in the theme editor and is visible after refreshing the page. [L1265.006]
- Place an opt-in bribe in the website footer, offering a free item in exchange for an email address. [L0757.012]
- Replace the default Shopify newsletter opt-in in the footer or sidebar with a giveaway offer. Keep the same form, change the text to name a valuable prize, and state the entry rules. [L0923.007]
- Add an exit-intent overlay to the website, which appears as a visitor is about to leave, to capture emails or promote offers. [L0757.013]
- Place the consultation or bribe offer next to the opt-in form on the static site. [L0514.017]

Opt-in formats by page, as a reference (older, 2021-22):

| Opt-in type | Where it works | Display format |
|---|---|---|
| Content opt-in | Every content page: blog posts, blog index, home page, collection pages | Sidebar, inline below content, exit-intent on all pages except product pages |
| Discount offer | Product pages only, first purchase only | Pop-up after about 30 seconds, exit-intent pop-up |
| Quiz | Any page | Home, collection and product pages as pop-up; collection, blog and blog post pages as sidebar item |
| Survey | Any page | Same placements as quiz |
| Contest or giveaway | Any page | Pop-up, exit-intent, or static sidebar form |
| Sales event opt-in | Any page | Pop-up or exit-intent; collection, blog and blog post pages as sidebar item; shown only in the one or two weeks before the event |

[L0571.040]

### Offer and opt-in copy

#### The bribe

- A bribe is a free item that visitors already want, given in exchange for their email address. Email is low-friction to share, but visitors only give it when they trust the offer and expect it to be respected. [L0514.010]
- Match the bribe to the stage of the visitor. Someone who has not chosen a niche yet responds to a list of niche ideas. Someone further along responds to a narrow piece of the paid product. [L0514.011]
- Pattern for a converting bribe: offer one narrow, specific snippet of the paid product that gives an immediate, concrete benefit as a free download. It works because the visitor gets proof of value before paying anything. [L0514.012]
  - Formula: `[specific free piece] + [what it solves] + [quick result]`
  - Example 1: A free one-page checklist that shows how to fix your morning routine to cut afternoon fatigue within three days.
  - Example 2: A free 15-minute video on the three fastest ways to lower your monthly phone bill this week.
  - [L0514.012]
- Pattern for a bribe headline: `[number of tips] + [specific fast result] + [timeframe]`. Specific numbers and a time limit make the free offer feel concrete and quick to use. [L0514.013]
  - Example 1: Seven budget meal prep ideas that help you cut 150 dollars from your grocery bill in four weeks.
  - Example 2: Five quick desk stretches that ease neck pain in ten days.
  - [L0514.013]
- Physical bribes, such as a free product with a shipping charge, are more expensive but produce more valuable leads. People who pay for shipping give their full name, email and shipping address, which signals strong purchase intent. [L0514.014]
- Case: a company marketed to preppers, people who expect a disaster and buy survival supplies. It offered a credit-card-sized folding knife as a free bribe, and customers paid shipping. The bribe was expensive, but the leads were very valuable because they came with shipping details and a paid order. [L0514.015]
- A free consultation, such as a 10-minute video call on a specific topic, works as a bribe when you have expertise but do not want to create a digital or physical giveaway. It costs your time per lead, so it suits low volumes. [L0514.016]
- The opt-in form collects a name and email address. Pair it with a catchy, specific headline that names the free bribe. [L0514.017]
- Quizzes, surveys and giveaways are covered in their own section below.

#### Opt-in copy

- Make every opt-in specific and valuable by naming the exact item the subscriber will receive. Vague promises such as "our newsletter and discounts" attract almost no one, because the visitor cannot judge the value. (Quote removed; vague promises such as `our newsletter and discounts` attract almost no one.) [L0571.003] (older, 2021-22)
- Each opt-in should solve one specific problem and promise one quick win, with one call to action. State the problem solved, the benefit and the time frame of delivery. [L0571.026] (older, 2021-22)
- A lead magnet performs best when it solves one specific problem with a specific solution for a specific segment of the market. Name the segment in the opt-in copy so the right visitors recognize the offer as meant for them. [L0571.031] (older, 2021-22)
- Formula: `[specific asset the visitor receives] + [the problem it solves] + [how and when it is delivered]`. Clear copy works because the visitor sees exactly what they get and can judge its value before entering an email address. [L0571.034] (older, 2021-22)
  - Example 1: `Enter your email and get our one-page checklist for recovering abandoned carts, sent to your inbox today.`
  - Example 2: `Join the list and receive our 12-page guide to choosing the right running shoe width, delivered instantly.`
  - [L0571.034] (older, 2021-22)
- Email signup headlines should match the brand voice, such as `Join Shaka Nation` or an equivalent branded call to action. Select a color scheme that matches your global theme to keep the design cohesive. [L0101.016]
- Shorter content opt-ins often outperform long ones. A checklist, how-to guide or infographic that solves one problem is easier to accept than a 100-page ebook, because people's time is limited and large downloads put them off. [L0571.010] (older, 2021-22)
- Content opt-in types include guides, PDF downloads of existing blog posts, infographics, ebooks, audio versions of content, checklists, cheat sheets, example collections, resource lists, inspiration files, recipe collections, calculators, spreadsheets, video courses, expert roundups and newsletters. Most can be reused from content already produced for promotion. [L0571.011] (older, 2021-22)
  - Formula: take a piece of content, identify the problem it solves, and offer it in a different format as a download behind an email field. [L0571.011] (older, 2021-22)
  - Example for an outdoor grill seller: `Get our top 10 ways to grill fish, with the recipes using our grill accessories, sent to your inbox.` [L0571.011] (older, 2021-22)
  - Example for a homeware seller: `Download 50 living room designs that feature the products we sell.` [L0571.011] (older, 2021-22)
- Sign-up success message: remove any line that tells the visitor to use the code to claim an offer, and state the code directly, for example with a line like `here is your discount code`. Keep the confirmation line about subscribing. [L0607.016]

### Lead magnets and content upgrades

- Lead generation procedure:
  1. Choose distribution channels for your free resource: blog banner ads, community sponsorships, Facebook ads, or partnerships with industry influencers.
  2. Build the free resource (eBook or report) addressing your audience's stated needs.
  3. Create a landing page to capture email addresses.
  4. Set up email automation in Klaviyo to deliver the free resource and begin the follow-up sequence.
  5. Import all captured contacts directly into Klaviyo for ongoing campaigns and weekly offers.
  - [L0240.006]
- Buyer's guide PDF procedure:
  1. Build the guide in Canva, using its brochure templates as a starting point, or in a word processor.
  2. Add the text, and drag in images created in Canva.
  3. Save as PDF: in Canva, choose PDF when saving; in a word processor, use File > Download as PDF.
  - [L0360.028]
- Before a buyer's guide download, use an exit-intent overlay in Klaviyo that asks for an email before the visitor leaves. Set the overlay text to invite the visitor to enter an email for the buyer's guide. [L1281.046] (older, 2022)
- Content upgrade template for a PDF version of a post (older, 2021-22):

```
Want a PDF version of this post? Enter your email below and we'll send you a copy you can save or print.
[Email field]
[Button: Download]
```
[L0571.037] (older, 2021-22)

- Content upgrade template for an audio version (older, 2021-22):

```
Prefer to listen? Get the audio version of this article: enter your email below and we'll send the download link straight away.
[Email field]
[Button: Get the audio]
```
[L0571.038] (older, 2021-22)

- A squeeze page is a simple opt-in page that asks for an email address in return for a free report or video. It removes ads, navigation tabs and every other distraction, so the visitor either subscribes or leaves. Its single goal is to convert visitors into subscribers. [L0523.078]

### Discount types and offer rules

#### Fixed amount versus percentage

- A fixed-amount dollar discount tends to convert better than a percentage discount for sign-up offers, because shoppers can see the exact value they receive. This is the author's experience, not a measured result. [L0634.003]
- A sign-up pop-up can use a fixed dollar amount instead of a percentage, such as `save $5 with your first order`, when a fixed amount feels like a clearer and bigger reward to the visitor. The matching discount code must be created in Shopify as a fixed amount with the same value, or the promise on the pop-up will not work at checkout. [L1297.046]
- Example from the lesson: a 5-dollar discount for an email address, with the headline changed to a question such as `Do you want 5 dollars off?` and a button reading `Sign up to get the deal`. The coupon is then created in Shopify Discounts as an Amount off order fixed amount of 5 dollars. [L0634.004]
- Percentage offers used in practice: 10% for an email sign-up and 15% for an SMS sign-up. [L0418.037] [L1138.001]
- Sources give no measured comparison between fixed and percentage offers. Use the fixed amount where the reward is clearer that way, and make sure the Shopify code matches the promise. [L0634.003] [L1297.046]

#### Channel-specific and first-purchase offers

- Use different discount offers by channel within one pop-up: 10% for email sign-ups and 15% for SMS sign-ups. Name the campaign so the offers are visible in the campaign name, which helps when comparing results later. [L1138.001]
- Discount opt-ins should be used only on product pages and only for the first purchase. Keep them time-limited, with windows such as 48 hours, 36 hours or one week, and do not make the window too long. Offer a meaningful to exceptional discount and deliver it by pop-up or exit-intent pop-up. [L0571.013] (older, 2021-22)
- Example (older, 2021-22): a discount opt-in on a product page shows a 15% off next-order offer with an email field, a `Be the first to know about new products` line and a `No thanks` option. [L0571.016]
- Welcome offer only for new leads: filter for `Amount spent equals zero dollars` so the welcome offer goes only to new leads, not previous customers who already know the brand. [L0201.010]
- Example: a merch store set the pop-up to display after 60 seconds, offered discount code `save 10`, and automated welcome emails to new subscribers while automatically excluding customers with abandoned cart tags and prior purchase history. [L0201.014]
- An exit-intent overlay should offer something different from the header discount. Do not repeat the same percentage in the overlay, since visitors already see it and have no reason to enter their email. A vague wording such as `enter your email for a special discount` also works. [L1090.071] (older, 2021)
- The Coupon Pop app for Shopify can show an expiring discount pop-up, such as 10 percent off, to collect email addresses. The same offer can stay in the store header, and the pop-up gives visitors a reason to leave their email instead of exiting. [L0923.006]
- Older rule (2020): an exit-intent overlay that offers a fixed discount or coupon in exchange for an email address is standard on large e-commerce sites. Giving a $50 coupon for signing up to a mailing list is legal. [L0967.076] (older, 2020)
- Offer must be stated clearly in the headline: a pop-up with a clear offer converts better than a prettier pop-up without one. [L0418.010]

### Timing, triggers and frequency

- Never show a pop-up or any overlay on page load. Do not show an email pop-up immediately on page load. [L1213.065] [L0607.011]
- Older rule (2021-22): do not show intrusive pop-ups or interstitials, including discount or newsletter pop-ups, within the first 30 seconds of a visit. [L1099.005] (older, 2021-22)
- Triggering immediately is penalized by Google, and discount pop-ups should be shown only to visitors who show real interest in the offer. [L0571.014] (older, 2021-22)
- Google can see page code loaded by pop-ups and interstitials that cover a significant part of the screen when it crawls a page, and may deprioritize such a page. Such overlays also reduce the chance that visitors stay. [L1099.007] (older, 2021-22)
- An interstitial is any overlay that covers part of the page, including discount or newsletter pop-ups and intrusive chat widgets that open as large bubbles. Keep both kinds off the screen during the first 30 seconds of a visit. [L1099.021] (older, 2021-22)
- Avoid showing the pop-up at the very start of the visit. [L0363.037]
- Showing the pop-up immediately when a visitor arrives annoys them and makes them leave. Let them scroll or stay on the page first. [L0605.041]
- A scroll-depth rule works as an engagement filter. A visitor who has read about half the page is more likely to want a discount or need reassurance, so the offer appears when interest is already shown. Pair the scroll rule with a delay of about 10 seconds and show the pop-up only when all selected rules are met. [L0607.049]

#### Trigger values by source

Sources disagree on the numbers. Use the value that matches the job:

| Source | Trigger | Condition | Use when | ids |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Bounced-visit pop-up | 60 seconds of visitor inactivity on the same page (no clicks or navigation) | Catches visitors before they close the tab | Visitor landed but took no action; they may be distracted, on a call, or have the site in a background tab | L0201.001, L0201.002 |
| Sign-up pop-up (Source C) | 10 seconds on the page plus 30-50% scroll; show only when all selected rules are met | Engagement filter | General sign-up pop-up | L0607.009, L0607.011, L0607.049, L0634.036 |
| Sign-up pop-up (variant) | 10-15 seconds on the page plus scrolling past half the page | Engagement filter | Klaviyo sign-up pop-up | L1213.065 |
| Klaviyo welcome trigger | About 15 seconds on the page, or the visitor has scrolled half the page | Not immediately on arrival | Welcome pop-up in Klaviyo | L0605.040 |
| Trigger logic (Source C) | Exit intent enabled, visitor has spent at least the store's average time on site, and 50% scroll | Example: 8 seconds average leads to showing it at 11 seconds | Pop-up that should appear only after real engagement | L0363.037, L0363.039 |
| Discount pop-up (older) | About 30 seconds on the page (speaker mentions 20-30 seconds) | Only for visitors who show real interest | Product-page discount opt-in | L0571.014 (older, 2021-22) |
| Mobile trigger (Source E) | Time-based trigger after 30 seconds on mobile; scroll trigger at 60% page scroll on both devices | Applied to both devices | Pop-up with separate mobile timing | L1138.003 |
| Exit intent (Source E) | On exit-intent, Device set to Desktop or Both devices | Visitor is about to leave | Last-chance pop-up | L1138.002, L1213.066 |

- Extra trigger: exit-intent pop-ups, which show when the visitor is about to leave, can be added to the welcome trigger. [L0605.040]
- Frequency rule (Source E): the pop-up shows a maximum of 2 times per visitor, with at least 1 hour between displays. Add it under Frequency > Add new rule. [L1138.004]

### Exit-intent overlays

- Exit-intent overlay: triggered when visitors show exit behavior, to capture visitors who would otherwise leave without engagement or conversion. [L0040.004]
- A bounced visit occurs when someone lands on the site but takes no action within a set time window. They may be distracted, on a call, or have the site open in a background tab. Interrupt them with a pop-up that exchanges a discount code for their email address, giving you another contact channel before they leave. [L0201.001]
- Configure the email pop-up to display after 60 seconds of visitor inactivity on the same page (no clicks or navigation) to catch them before they close the tab. [L0201.002]
- Page coverage conflicts between sources:
  - Course A-style setup: the email pop-up displays on all page types: homepage, blog posts, collection pages and product pages. Use this when the goal is maximum capture across the store. [L0201.003]
  - Older rule (2021-22): exit-intent on all pages except product pages. Use this when the product page should stay free of overlays. [L0571.007] (older, 2021-22)
- Setup via the bounce visits module: use Module 5.7 (Bounce visits) to set up an exit-intent overlay that captures visitor email addresses before they leave the store. [L0208.008]
- Setup procedure in Source E's pop-up tool: go to Trigger > Add new trigger and choose `On exit-intent` (`When a visitor is about to leave your site`). Set Device to Desktop or Both devices. Exit-intent catches visitors who leave without converting and works on both mobile and desktop. [L1138.002]
- Use an exit-intent pop-up as the last chance to catch a visitor who is about to leave the site. Show it only when the visitor moves to leave. [L1213.066]
- An exit pop is a last-chance pop-up that appears when a visitor tries to leave without subscribing. It can include a special offer for subscribing. Since departing visitors rarely return, it is acceptable to be a little intrusive here. [L0523.083]
- Do not run two exit-intent pop-ups at once, such as one from the theme and another from Klaviyo. Keep one and turn off the other. [L0855.116] (2023)
- Manual follow-up for opt-ins: if no exit-intent overlay is running, set one up. Manually email everyone who opts in (not automated messages) with the following text, which builds the relationship and recovers sales:

```
I'm the owner, noticed you on our site, happy to help with any questions.
```
[L0274.017] (2023)

### Pop-up design

#### Visual and layout rules

- Baseline case: a starting pop-up with a 0.4% conversion rate and 16,000 views produced only 71 email submissions. This baseline shows the value of a stronger visual pop-up design. [L0418.006]
- Use a lighter pop-up tool than the Klaviyo embed (OptiMonk was chosen for this reason) because heavier pop-up scripts slow page load time, and page speed affects conversion. [L0418.007]
- Make pop-up buttons and inputs very clickable: use display manual with a fixed width of about 400 px and a height of 50 to 70 px, and keep buttons larger than inputs on mobile. [L0418.008]
- Match the pop-up button and input shape, color and shadow to the store's own product-page buttons so the pop-up feels like part of the site. [L0418.009]
- Set the floating product image and text to be legible and eye-catching. A floating image breaking out of the pop-up frame attracts attention, and a text shadow improves readability on busy backgrounds. [L0418.011]
- The image on a sign-up pop-up should relate to the niche or the product, or show a gift, because it sits beside the discount offer and supports it. Replace the default image in the Styles section before publishing the form. [L0607.054]
- Check every pop-up on mobile view and adjust sizes separately for desktop and mobile, because the layout, button size and font size often need different values on each device. [L0418.012]
- Check the sign-up form on a mobile preview before publishing. Most visitors in a mobile-heavy store arrive from phones, and a form that looks fine on desktop can be hard to use on a phone. [L0607.012]
- Pure black on a pop-up background can look fake and digital next to a website. A dark charcoal tone that blends into the site looks more natural, so pick the shade by eye beside the store's own colors. [L0418.027]

#### OptiMonk settings

- Layering: give the email input fields and the Reveal Code button a higher z-index (about 10) than the floating image (about 1), so the overlay image does not capture clicks. [L0418.013]
- Padding: zero out any leftover column spacing first, then open Column > Advanced and set left and right padding to 0 and top and bottom padding to 50 px. If the result looks cramped or too loose, test 100 px on top and bottom and keep the value that looks more even. [L0418.023]
- Text shadow: set text shadow under Style > Text shadows, using a medium shadow on all text blocks. A light shadow looked too weak on a sandy background, and applying the same shadow everywhere keeps readability consistent. [L0418.024]
- Hero image: for a full-width hero background, OptiMonk's image fit options under Style > Image are cover, contain and original, with an alignment choice that can be adjusted. In the tested pop-up the original fit looked most natural, so compare fit modes visually before settling on one. [L0418.025]
- Container shadow: give the whole pop-up container a soft shadow with a medium size and about 30% opacity so it lifts off the page. Keep the opacity low, because a heavy shadow makes the pop-up look dated. [L0418.026]
- Mobile layout procedure:
  1. Switch the OptiMonk editor to mobile view and review the pop-up before changing anything.
  2. Select the floating image, go to Display > Position, and set it to Top.
  3. Select the column and set left and right padding to 0, then add back about 25 px if the content touches the edges.
  4. Select Main page and set its size to 95% of the screen.
  5. Set mobile inputs to a fixed width of about 400 px and a height of about 50 px, and set the button to about 60 to 70 px high.
  6. Raise any headline or button text that is too small on mobile; the tested pop-up used sizes around 125 to 150.
  7. Adjust the desktop and mobile layouts separately, because they often need different values.
  - [L0418.028]
- Preview caveat: a pop-up preview may not show on some themes, and an OptiMonk pop-up does not appear until the app embed is enabled in the Shopify theme settings. Check that the embed is on before assuming the pop-up is broken. [L0418.014]

### Pop-up tools and platform setup

#### Choosing a tool

- Tools for pop-ups, exit-intent forms, inline forms on content pages and sidebar forms include OptinMonster and OptiMonk. Other options include Sumo and Just You Know. [L0608.006] (older, 2021-22)
- OptinMonster is used for all pop-ups, including exit-intent, timed, inline forms and opt-ins, and it integrates with Shopify. [L0682.017] (older, 2021-22)
- The key criterion when choosing a provider is its impact on page speed, because some scripts load slowly and hurt site performance. Privy is named as an example of a slow tool with problematic script loading, so avoid it or switch away from it. [L0608.007] (older, 2021-22)
- Built-in forms from email platforms such as Klaviyo are weaker than dedicated form tools, because email software is not built around forms and pop-ups and those features feel like an afterthought. A separate specialized form tool is therefore preferred. [L0608.008] (older, 2021-22)
- Conflict: the 2021-22 advice prefers a dedicated tool, while the Source C method builds the sign-up pop-up in Klaviyo. Use Klaviyo's sign-up form when it meets the page-speed test, and use OptiMonk when the Klaviyo embed is too heavy. [L0608.008] [L0607.005] [L0418.007] (older for the first)
- Pop-ups can still collect email addresses if used carefully, although ad blockers and some ad networks reject ads that send traffic to pages with pop-ups. Newer pop-up formats are harder to block than older ones. Check the advertising platform's policy before relying on them. [L0523.077]

#### Klaviyo sign-up form

- Choose Pop-up, not Full page or Flyout, because pop-up has the highest conversion. Choose Email only as the form's channel and select the Newsletter list so new addresses land in the newsletter list. [L0607.005]
- Teaser: keep the teaser off by default, because a teaser that stays at the bottom of the screen annoys mobile visitors. Turn it on only when the sign-up form conversion rate drops. [L0607.006]
- Targeting: under Show based on rules, show the pop-up after 10 seconds on the page, and add the rule that the visitor has scrolled 30 to 50 percent of the page. Set the condition to show only when all selected rules are met. [L0607.009]
- Creation procedure:
  1. Click Create, choose the email-only option and select the Newsletter list.
  2. Choose the Pop-up form type and click Next.
  3. Set the teaser to off, unless the sign-up rate drops.
  4. Choose a static coupon for the incentive and enter the code created in Shopify (for example WELCOME10).
  5. Open Edit form and adjust the layout; check the mobile version, because most visitors come from mobile devices.
  6. Open Styles to change the image, text and styling options.
  7. Open Targeting and set the rules (see the targeting setting above).
  8. Edit the copy: headline, email field text, button text and decline link.
  9. Open the success step, remove the line about claiming the offer and add a line that gives the discount code directly.
  10. Change the status from Draft to Live, then click Exit.
  11. Preview the form on a mobile device before finishing.
  - [L0607.008]
- Mistake: showing the sign-up pop-up the moment a page loads is too aggressive and annoys many visitors. A delay of about 10 seconds combined with a scroll condition gives better results. [L0607.011]
- Coupon pop-up copy: use the Klaviyo success message and the Shopify code as described above. [L0607.016] [L0607.008]

#### Targeting, segments and reuse

- Targeting: under Select who should see the pop-up, choose a predefined segment such as `Non-subscribers` so people already on the list do not see it. Other predefined options include Now visitors, Active shoppers, All visitors, Engaged visitors, New home page visitors, Just purchased and Custom targeting. Saved segments and Copy from previous campaign are also available. [L1138.005]
- In the targeting step, Load segment opens your saved segments, and Save as segment stores the rule you built so it can be reused in other pop-up campaigns. Copy from previous campaign reuses targeting from an existing pop-up. Save a segment when the same audience rule is needed across several pop-ups. [L1138.017]
- Welcome pop-ups can be limited to new visitors only, but this requires a custom code addition in the theme. [L1094.069] (2023)

### Shopify automation after capture

- Set up Shopify Email automation triggered by bounced-visit pop-up submissions. It automatically sends captured contacts a coupon code while simultaneously forwarding their contact information to your email for manual follow-up. [L0154.003]
- Module 5.7 (Bounce visits) is the setup for the exit-intent overlay that feeds this automation. [L0208.008]
- Captured emails feed abandoned cart and promotion emails. [L0468.088] (older, 2020)
- Over time, feed contacts from exit-intent overlays into automated flows. [L1092.083] (older, 2022)
- Remarketing: visitors who leave can be remarketed to once their contact details are captured. [L1002.033] (older, 2019)

### Phone capture and multi-step forms

- An extra incentive, such as an additional 5% off, can be offered in exchange for a phone number. This works because the visitor is already looking for a better deal when they try to leave. [L0363.038]
- Trojan Horse Method for collecting email and phone number in one pop-up:
  1. Ask for the email address first.
  2. Once the email is captured, offer the visitor the option to upgrade by also adding a phone number.
  3. If the visitor adds a phone number, give them the upgraded offer, which is a discount double the basic one.
  4. If the visitor declines, keep only the email and send the basic offer they signed up for.
  - [L0494.003]
- Asking for the email first guarantees the email capture, while the optional phone upgrade lets the visitor choose their own reward path. The basic sign-up is never blocked by the phone request. [L0494.004]
- Two-step form: step one collects a name and email address, which qualifies the visitor as a subscriber even if they leave. Step two collects credit card details to cover shipping in a free-plus-shipping offer. [L0523.080]
- Case: a Klaviyo pop-up on a store with about 16,000 views produced only 71 email sign-ups, a conversion rate of about 0.4%. The rebuild used an OptiMonk pop-up with a floating product image, a 10% email discount and a follow-up SMS upgrade to 15% with TCPA-style consent text under the button. The reported result was over 30,000 USD in under 10 days. This is a self-reported figure and not independently verified. [L0418.037]

### Quizzes, surveys, contests and giveaways

#### Quizzes

- Quizzes are a pre-frame tool that engages visitors with personal questions that point toward the offer. Use three to four numbered questions, such as `Step 1 of 4`, to show progress, although some successful quizzes run to 20 questions. Requiring an email address to see the results combines the pre-frame with subscriber qualification. [L0523.068]
- A quiz can segment the audience by answers. Asking whether a visitor owns a dog or a cat splits the list, and a dog owner can then be asked how old the dog is and shown answers by age range. The next ad can then show a dog and recommend food suited to that dog's age. [L0523.069]
- Quizzes and surveys can run on any page: home page, product pages, collection pages and blog posts, as pop-ups or as fixed items such as sidebar or inline forms. Home, collection and product placements suit pop-ups; collection, blog and blog post pages suit sidebar items. Each quiz or survey should have its own list. [L0571.017] (older, 2021-22)
- Quizzes and surveys work because they are fun or engaging and carry no pressure to buy, so they can reach very high opt-in rates. The quiz does not have to be about the products; it only has to relate to the niche and interest the visitor. Example: a surfboard seller can use `Which world surf pro are you?` and later move these subscribers toward surfboard products. [L0571.018] (older, 2021-22)
- Tools: Typeform was used to build quizzes and surveys in the course. Qzzr (Quizzer) is an alternative for quizzes. Google Forms is a simple alternative for surveys. Quizzes run on the page or in a lightbox pop-up, and the visitor enters an email address at the end to see the results. [L0571.019] (older, 2021-22)
- Quiz formula: `Which [recognizable figure or category from your niche] are you?` with a short quiz that the visitor finishes to see the result. The question invites self-identification and fun, so visitors give their email to see the answer even when the quiz does not mention products. [L0571.036] (older, 2021-22)
  - Example 1: `Which camping style matches your next trip?`
  - Example 2: `Which coffee brewing method fits your morning routine?`
  - [L0571.036] (older, 2021-22)
- Keep a quiz short, with only a few simple inputs before the email step, so visitors finish quickly. The email request should come after the quiz has already delivered some engagement. [L0571.039] (older, 2021-22)
- A quiz works as a lead magnet when the results are revealed only after the visitor enters an email address. This turns the quiz into a fast way to grow the list with people who searched for related products. [L0922.018] (older, 2021-22)
- A quiz built to capture emails can be linked from several places on the store, such as collection pages and the home page, not only from ads. For paid search, use a dedicated quiz page as the final URL so the ad headline and the landing page match. [L0922.026] (older, 2021-22)

#### Surveys

- A survey asks visitors questions about their industry and preferences. Turn the results into a content piece such as `We surveyed 100 people in this industry`, which is likely to be shared. Send each respondent the results before publishing so they see how their views compare. [L0571.020] (older, 2021-22)

#### Contests and giveaways

- Small contests and giveaways generate large numbers of opt-ins. The prize does not need to be the main product; cheap items that sell for around $100 can be bought and given away. Offer contests on any page through pop-ups, exit-intent pop-ups or sidebar forms, and promote them on social media to grow entries. [L0571.021] (older, 2021-22)
- Giveaway prizes can be set so that one prize is awarded for every set number of opt-ins, such as one prize per 1,000 opt-ins. Even a 500 dollar prize can remain profitable under this rule, because collected emails are cheap to market to later. [L0923.008]
- Giveaway opt-in text for the newsletter box. Replace the placeholders in square brackets:

```
Enter your email to win [PRIZE, for example a 200 dollar gift card to our store or our best-selling product] at [STORE NAME]. We will give away one [PRIZE] for every [NUMBER, for example 1,000] people who opt in. Full rules: [LINK OR TEXT WITH RULES].
```
[L0923.009]

- Replace the default newsletter opt-in with a giveaway offer that names a valuable prize, such as a 200 dollar gift card or your best-selling product, and states the entry rules. [L0923.007]

### Sales event and other capture formats

- Sales event opt-ins offer presale or exclusive access to a big sales event such as Black Friday, Easter or Veterans Day. They can appear as pop-up or exit-intent on any page, or as sidebar items on collection, blog and blog post pages. Each sales event should have its own list. Show them only in the one or two weeks before the event. [L0571.022] (older, 2021-22)
- Squeeze pop: a button placed in blog posts or articles that opens a squeeze-style pop-up when clicked. A visitor who enters an email address moves to the next page. Squeeze pops work anywhere a button can be placed, including places where normal opt-in forms would not fit. [L0523.079]
- Free account: gives visitors access to a membership or a lite version of your software in exchange for signing up. Free accounts are similar to squeeze pages but collect more personal information, such as a physical address and phone number, while keeping conversions high. Use them for software and membership products. [L0523.082]
- Webinar registration is a natural way to collect email addresses, because registrants expect follow-up emails with details. Automated webinars let people pick the day they want to watch, and after entering an email address they become subscribers. Registrants are also primed to look for your emails. [L0523.081]
- Consultation as a bribe: see the bribe section above. [L0514.016]

### Page speed and technical considerations

- Choose the pop-up tool by its effect on page speed, not only its features. [L0608.007] (older, 2021-22)
- Use a lighter pop-up script than the Klaviyo embed where page speed is a concern, because page speed affects conversion. [L0418.007]
- Check that the pop-up embed is enabled before assuming a pop-up is broken. [L0418.014]
- Overlays that cover a significant part of the screen can be seen by Google and reduce the chance that visitors stay. Keep overlays off the first 30 seconds of a visit. [L1099.007] [L1099.005] (older, 2021-22)
- Pop-ups and overlays can be blocked by ad blockers and some ad networks. Check the ad platform's policy before sending paid traffic to pop-up pages. [L0523.077]
- Measurement: use Lucky Orange, a screen-recording app, to watch visitor sessions and find the average time on site needed for pop-up timing. [L0363.039]
- Do not add Google reCAPTCHA to an email sign-up form to block spam, because it can reduce opt-in rates. Monitor new sign-ups and delete obvious spam manually. [L0836.010] (2024)

### Compliance and consent

- Give a fixed-amount or percentage coupon only where the platform terms and local rules allow it. Giving a $50 coupon for signing up to a mailing list is legal. [L0967.076] (older, 2020)
- SMS upgrades carry TCPA-style consent text under the button. [L0418.037]
- Ads and pop-up policies: check the advertising platform's policy before relying on pop-ups in paid campaigns. [L0523.077]

#### Further details

- Portugal candles brand: €1,000 total Black Friday ad budget for France market. Used list-building strategy with landing page opt-ins. Achieved €2.89 cost per lead. After €3,000 total investment, still acquiring leads at this low cost, demonstrating that list building remains profitable even when purchase CPCs spike to €20+. [L0307.008]

- Set the pop-up targeting to Show based on rules with two triggers: exit intent (when the visitor is leaving the page) and a delay of 10 seconds after the page loads. The delay gives visitors time to scroll before the offer appears. [L0476.005]

## Recovering lost visitors and carts

### Abandoned checkout versus abandoned cart

- An abandoned cart means items were added but checkout was never started. An abandoned checkout means the customer started checkout and did not finish, whether because of a declined payment, incomplete information, or an interruption. [L0266.001]
- Abandoned checkout recovery is more effective than abandoned cart recovery because these customers have progressed further in the purchase journey. [L0266.001]
- Abandoned checkout recovery automatically follows up with customers who add products, start checkout, and leave without completing the purchase. It recovers sales that would otherwise be lost while purchase intent is still high, and it runs without daily management. [L0149.001]
- An abandoned checkout workflow triggers when checkout is abandoned with a total greater than $0. Customers who complete their purchase exit the workflow. [L0149.002]
- An abandoned cart flow is an automated sequence that follows up with visitors who entered checkout but did not complete the purchase, targeting high-intent prospects in recovery. [L0040.005]
- Exit-intent overlays and abandoned cart flows address two distinct drop-off points: visitors who leave without acting, and prospects who abandon checkout. Each needs a separate strategy. [L0040.008]

### Setting up the abandoned checkout automation

1. Open Marketing > Automations in the Shopify admin. Automations are created through the Messaging app but edited in the Automations section. [L0282.004]
2. Build the workflow using the conditions in the table below. [L0149.002, L0266.002, L0266.005, L0266.006, L0266.007, L0266.009, L0266.011, L0282.018]
3. Confirm the workflow is active. The status button shows `Turn off workflow` when it is active. If it shows `Turn on workflow`, the automation is not yet active. [L0149.011]
4. Once set up, the automation runs in perpetuity and executes each time a customer abandons a checkout. It can be modified or paused at any time without re-setup. [L0282.002]

| Element | Setting | Source |
|---|---|---|
| Trigger | Checkout abandoned with a total greater than $0 | L0149.002, L0266.005 |
| Stock check | Stock status is verified before the first email. The email does not send if the product is no longer in inventory. | L0266.007 |
| Exit check | Before each email, the system checks whether the customer has placed an order or started another checkout since abandonment. If either occurred, the customer is removed from the flow. | L0266.006 |
| First email | Sent after a 4-hour initial delay | L0282.018 |
| Second email wait | Second wait timer set to 20 hours after the first email. Combined with the 4-hour delay, the second email is sent 24 hours after the original abandonment. | L0282.018 |
| Second email condition | Sent only if the customer's total amount spent equals $0 | L0266.009 |
| Customer tag | Add the customer tag `abandoned cart` to all contacts entering the workflow. Other automations reference this tag to exclude these customers and prevent duplicate messages. | L0266.011 |
| New subscribers | Enable only the abandoned checkout automation, not the abandoned cart automation. New subscribers already receive welcome emails, so abandoned cart emails create redundant contact. | L0266.002 |

- The 4-hour first-email delay [L0282.018] falls outside the one-hour window cited in [L0261.003]. The sources do not reconcile these figures. The table follows the setup described in [L0282.018].

### Speed of follow-up

- Customers who open the abandoned cart email within the first hour after abandonment are 400% more likely to purchase. [L0261.003]
- Speed is critical because customers stay mentally engaged with the purchase for only minutes. Beyond 30 minutes, distractions shift focus, memory of the purchase fades, and competitors can capture the sale. [L0281.003]
- Where possible, call the customer as soon as possible after abandonment, because speed matters most. [L0471.001]

### Manual follow-up of abandoned checkouts

Procedure A (2024):

1. Open the Shopify admin, go to Orders, then Abandoned checkouts. [L0471.001]
2. Count how many abandoned checkouts contain an email address or phone number. [L0471.001]
3. Send each of those customers a manual follow-up email. [L0471.001]
4. Where possible, call them as soon as possible after they abandon, because speed matters most. [L0471.001]

Procedure B (2023):

1. Go to Shopify > Orders > Abandoned Carts. [L0274.016]
2. Follow up with all abandoned cart customers via email, phone, or SMS. [L0274.016]
3. Message: `Noticed you started checkout, happy to help if you have questions.` [L0274.016]
4. This can recover 1-3 quick sales. [L0274.016]

- The Abandoned checkouts section lists customers who started checkout but did not complete the order. Checking this list regularly and following up is a way to recover lost sales. [L1302.010]

### Phone and SMS follow-up

- When calling an abandoned checkout customer, use this template. It treats the abandonment as a checkout problem rather than disinterest, which increases answer rates and conversion. [L0286.011]

```
Hi [customer name], I saw you were looking at a [product name] that would ship to [city/state]. You didn't complete your checkout-how can I help you finish the order?
```

- Follow-up can run by email, phone, or SMS. [L0274.016]
- Checkout contact setting: go to Settings > Checkout > Customer contact. Set the field to email address only if SMS retargeting is not working. Customers can then only enter an email at checkout, and the store can retarget them with Klaviyo. [L1216.012]
- During a paid testing phase, always keep SMS Bump or an equivalent SMS flow active. Checkout drop-offs with a phone number can be retargeted by SMS. [L1216.013]
- Without SMS Bump, collect email addresses at checkout so email retargeting can be used instead. [L1216.013]

### Real-time alerts and tracking

Procedure:

1. Send automatic follow-up emails to customers with abandoned carts. [L0236.002]
2. Display targeted Facebook ads to the customer. [L0236.002]
3. Send a real-time notification including customer name, cart value, phone number, item details, and cart link. [L0236.002]
4. Log customer information to a Google Sheet for tracking. [L0236.002]

- Abandoned cart notification messages must include: customer name, total cart value, customer contact phone number, specific items in the cart, and a direct link to the abandoned cart. [L0236.003]
- Real-time Slack alerts add to email follow-ups and Facebook retargeting. They do not replace them. Using all three together maximizes recovery. [L0281.007]
- Recovery tracking: create a second Zapier automation that checks incoming paid orders against the abandoned cart sheet in Google Sheets. If the customer appears in both, update the sheet with the recovery date and method (phone call, email, etc.). [L0286.012]

### Diagnosing clicks without abandoned checkouts

Procedure (2025):

1. In Shopify, open Orders and click Abandoned checkouts. [L0837.009]
2. If abandoned checkouts exist, email each customer personally from the owner's address, referencing the product and offering help. [L0837.009]
3. If there are none despite many clicks, check which keywords and search terms triggered the clicks in Google Ads. [L0837.009]
4. Adjust ad spend based on what the search terms show. [L0837.009]

### Why visitors leave

- Abandoned checkout visitors show high purchase intent but did not buy. The usual reasons are the overall offer (not only price, but any discount, bonus item, or ebook), the product price, and trust, since first-time visitors may doubt the store or be unsure whether to buy now. [L0605.042]
- Common reasons for cart abandonment are high or unexpected shipping costs, complicated shipping rates or policies, and distractions such as a doorbell, a phone call, or a notification. Distraction cases are solved mainly by reminders. [L1208.006]
- Visitors who do not buy immediately are unlikely to return directly. The speaker estimated that about 90% will not bookmark the store and will instead search again and click a competitor or a paid ad, which costs more money. Close the sale on the first visit. [L1293.014]

### Reminders, retargeting, and second chances

- Visitors who leave without buying, and abandoned carts, should be followed up later with reminders so they can be reached once they are ready to buy. [L0582.012]
- Pop-up offers, flow emails, and SMS reminders are each a second chance to convert. Traffic from paid ads or organic content without retargeting messages is largely wasted when visitors leave without buying. [L0634.033]
- Conversion rates increase when customers interact with the website multiple times. Retargeting and multi-touch strategies are therefore essential to move visitors closer to purchase. [L0257.005]
- Set up remarketing from the start with a small budget, around a few dollars per day, so visitors who did not buy can be reached again. (Older advice, 2020.) [L0468.089]

### Exit-intent visitors

Procedure (older advice, 2022):

1. Check how many visitors entered their email on the exit-intent overlay. [L0963.061]
2. Email each person personally with an introduction as the owner. [L0963.061]
3. Invite a reply or call and offer help choosing the right product. [L0963.061]

### Abandoned cart email content

- Test changes to live flow emails through split tests whenever traffic allows. A low-traffic store cannot test reliably, so keep changes small and treat results as weak evidence. [L0567.015]
- Subject line effectiveness varies by audience and product type. Test formulas (question, how-to, scarcity, declaration, numbers, curiosity, personalized) across successive sends and compare open rates. (Older advice, 2021-22.) [L0198.018]
- Open the first abandoned cart email with the logo, because the message must build trust before it asks the shopper to return to checkout. [L0567.016]
- Place the company logo at the top of every email in the same form it uses on the website, so readers know they are in the right place. (Older advice, 2021-22.) [L0569.016]
- Generous spacing between blocks makes the email easier to scan. Avoid crowding sections. Changing the email width matters little compared with clean spacing. [L0567.017]
- Show third-party ratings as trust icons, for example a 4.9-star rating on Google and Trustpilot and a 5-star rating on a review site. Shoppers check reviews before buying, so placing the proof in the email saves them the search. [L0567.018]
- Test one to three customer testimonials as social proof, and import reviews automatically with Klaviyo Reviews where the account is connected. The testimonial test is planned as the next iteration of the email. [L0567.019]
- Answer the "why" with specific relevance to this customer rather than generic benefits. Specificity makes the email feel personally relevant rather than broadcast. Example: `We understand keeping your gear comfortable during long rides is what matters to you.` instead of `This product is great.` [L0261.021]
- USP pattern that also handles an objection: choose the selling point that answers a common doubt, so one line does two jobs. Shipping speed answers "when will it arrive". Own example: `Order before 2pm, shipped the same day` answers the delivery-time doubt. [L0567.021]
- Risk-reduction email pattern: give a review count and star rating as proof, invite the shopper to buy with confidence, then restate the return promise. Own examples: `Over 2,000 five-star reviews from riders across the country. Buy with confidence.` and `Rated 4.8 by 900 home cooks: try it risk-free.` [L0567.025]
- The risk-reduction email can show secure payment options as payment badges and point to social proof such as Instagram stories and customer reviews. Together these reassure a shopper worried about scams before they return to checkout. [L0567.049]

### Welcome series and personal outreach

- Manually email every new subscriber within one business day as the store owner. Personal outreach converts better than automation alone. [L0201.015]
- Structure in four parts: (1) acknowledge their signup, (2) introduce yourself as owner and briefly state your store's stage or focus, (3) ask what they need or whether they have questions, (4) invite them to reply directly. [L0201.015]
- Example 1: `Hi, just saw your signup. I'm [name], founder here. We focus on [category]-what brought you in?` [L0201.015]
- Example 2: `I'm [name], owner of [store]. Noticed you joined our list. What got your attention-any specific products or questions?` [L0201.015]
- Each email must feel personal and vary by subscriber. Do not include standard unsubscribe links or brand signatures. The goal is to start real conversations and learn what visitors want. [L0201.015]
- Add urgency and scarcity in the final emails of a welcome series, such as a countdown to offer expiry. This pushes subscribers who have not bought yet to act before the deadline. [L1470.021]
- Abandoned cart emails should run as a sequence in Klaviyo to recover checkouts that were not finished. (Older advice, 2022.) [L1092.084]

### Reorder pages

- Reorder landing pages make it easy for existing customers to buy the same products again. The vendor presents them as a way to build repeat purchases and loyalty after the first order. [L0593.008]

## Live chat, forms and customer contact

### Why live chat and fast answers matter

- Live chat removes friction during the buying decision. It lets customers ask questions instantly, helps you close sales in real time, and is a more convenient alternative to contact forms or email. [L0151.002]
- Live chat leads to extra sales because shoppers get answers in real time instead of waiting hours for an email reply. For a store built this way, live chat is almost a standard feature. [L0894.015]
- Live chat lets visitors with a question get an answer while still on the site, instead of searching for an email address, filling in a contact form and waiting. Answering in near real time can close sales that would otherwise be lost, so live chat matters from the early stage of a store. [L1311.003]
- A live chat button on the store lets shoppers ask last-minute questions on product pages. This recovers sales that would otherwise be lost, and real-time answers at the moment of doubt are the core benefit. [L0948.002]
- Shoppers expect fast answers and fast delivery, so a visible way to get instant responses helps convert browsers into buyers. Competitors offering next-day delivery make slow answers and 2-3 day shipping a conversion risk. [L1399.003]
- A live chat widget, for example the Tidio Live Chat app, lets visitors ask questions and get answers immediately while browsing. This turns browsers into buyers, since shoppers expect instant answers and fast delivery. [L1400.003]

### Choosing a chat tool

| Tool | What the source says | Source ids |
|---|---|---|
| Tidio | Live chat app for Shopify stores that lets you answer customer questions in real time. [L1358.022] Used in the setup procedure below. [L0635.022] | L1358.022, L1400.003 |
| Shopify Inbox | Shopify's built-in chat tool for answering quick customer questions on the store. [L1358.023] Free live chat that can replace paid chat tools. It supports pre-written answers triggered by keywords and is moving toward AI chat. [L1283.074] | L1358.023, L1283.074 |
| tawk.to (Talk2) | Older (2021-2022): a recommended online chat option for Shopify. [L0682.019] | L0682.019 |
| Gorgias | Older (2021-2022): a more integrated and more expensive alternative for chat than tawk.to. [L0682.019] | L0682.019 |

- Shopify Inbox is the free option. Tidio, tawk.to and Gorgias are paid or freemium tools, and Gorgias is the most integrated and most expensive of them. [L1283.074, L0682.019]

### Setting up Tidio

1. Install Tidio from the Shopify App Store. [L0635.022]
2. Turn on the automations so the chat answers basic questions such as pricing and delivery times automatically. [L0635.022]
3. Customize the widget appearance. [L0635.022]
4. Under Chatbot, turn on the Welcome series under Increase sales so visitors receive a welcome message. [L0635.022]
5. Under Increase sales, open Cart abandoned and use a template. [L0635.022]
6. Set the discount in the abandoned cart flow. The video changes 15 to 10 percent. Accept, activate, choose a coupon such as WELCOME10 with a value of 10, and save. [L0635.022]

### Chat widget design and placement

- In the theme customizer, open the App embeds section to change the chat widget. Options include the widget color, text color, button color, and the icon (chat bubble, smiley face or hand wave), with position set to bottom right and the lowest vertical position. [L1311.007]
- Pick the icon and colors that fit the site, since no single option is better. [L1311.007]
- A widget color that contrasts with the site's palette makes the chat icon easier to notice, which increases the chance of a click. Icon style is mostly a matter of brand fit and taste. [L1311.008]
- Once chat is active, the chat icon appears on every page of the storefront, so visitors can start a conversation from any page. [L1311.009]
- Older (2021-2022): chat widgets must not open as a large bubble that covers more than a third of the screen on mobile. Configure them to open only after the visitor has read part of the page or has stayed on the page for a set time. [L1099.006]

### Chat greeting

- The greeting shown next to the chat icon can be edited in the widget settings, and a preview updates as the text changes. The default text is a generic greeting that invites questions and promises help. [L1311.010]
- Formula for a chat welcome message:
  - Name the store.
  - Invite the visitor to ask a specific question.
  - Add a short reassurance of a quick reply.
  - Keep it to one or two short sentences so it is easy to read in a small widget and gives the visitor a clear reason to click. [L1311.011]
- Example 1: [L1311.011]

```
Welcome to [Store Name]! Ask us anything about sizes, shipping or your order.
```

- Example 2: [L1311.011]

```
Hi, this is [Store Name]. Have a question before you buy? Send it over and we will reply shortly.
```

### Capturing customer details in chat

Two sources give different rules for when to ask for contact details. Use the rule that matches your response speed.

- **Email before chat (Shopify Inbox):** Shopify Inbox asks for the customer's email before chat. Keeping the email field is acceptable because people who ask buying questions usually give their email. The trade-off is fewer chat starts. [L0856.037]
- **Capture when you cannot reply quickly:** Live chat should capture the customer's name and email if you cannot reply within a minute or two, so you can follow up later. [L1283.073]
- **No capture when you reply quickly:** If you can respond quickly from your phone, this capture is not necessary. [L1283.073]
- **Which to use:** Keep the email gate if chat starts matter less than lead quality. Use the name and email capture only when a reply within a minute or two is not realistic. Drop it when you can answer from your phone in that window. [L0856.037, L1283.073]

### Recommendations in chat

- Personalized product recommendations given in chat, based on a shopper's stated needs such as skin type, can raise conversion rate and create upsell opportunities. The AI agent can suggest a fitting product directly in the conversation. [L0753.004]

### Response speed

- Older (2020): Answer live chat, phone calls and emails quickly, because a slow reply can lose a sale that is worth $1,000 or more in net profit over a month. [L0468.090]
- Older (2022): Customer service responsiveness affects conversions. When customers call or email with questions and receive no reply, they abandon the purchase. Monitor inquiries and respond quickly. [L0272.066]
- Older (2022): Early on, a store's conversion rate is driven by manual follow-up with every inquiry. Even one or two extra sales from inquiries can put a young store in profit. [L1092.082]
- Older (2022): In the first sales phase, handle every inbound contact manually. Answer the phone quickly, call back missed calls, and reply to every inquiry with real effort. This personal touch drives high conversion for a new store. [L0963.060]
- Make the response fast and convenient, because even an interested reader will procrastinate when responding costs effort. Offer a toll-free number, consider staffing it around the clock, and consider fax, website or email response options. [L1370.093]

### Quote forms for trade and bulk buyers

- Custom quote forms built with Wufoo or Typeform trigger Zapier to send a private Slack message to the sales team and auto-populate a Google Sheets quote tracker. Respond to inquiries within minutes to maximize quote-to-order conversion. [L0286.018]
- Quote reply template: [L0360.025]

```
Hi [customer name],

Thank you so much for your inquiry. We work with many [customer type, e.g. interior designers] and we are more than happy to provide custom quotes for your business.

I am the owner of [store name]. Please reply to this email with the products you are interested in and the quantities, and I will get back to you with a quote.

Best regards,
[your name]
[store name]
```

- Trade program welcome template: [L0360.026]

```
Welcome to our trade program. We love working with [customer type]. If you ever need special orders or bulk orders, please email [trade email alias, e.g. trade@yourstore.com] or call [phone number].
```

### Phone numbers on the site

- Showing a phone number on the store tends to raise the conversion rate, even when visitors never call. It signals that the business can be reached and reduces doubt before buying. [L0700.001]
- Add a phone number on the store's contact page. Customers rarely call, but its presence builds trust, and the few who call are more likely to buy. [L0359.060]
- Place the business phone number in three spots: [L0700.012]
  - the header, which shows it on every page;
  - the footer, for visitors who look for contact details;
  - the contact page, for visitors who look for contact details.
- Older (2021): Set a voicemail message that thanks the caller, says you are away or helping another customer, and asks for a name, email address and inquiry. Even if you rarely answer the phone, the number builds trust and makes visitors more likely to buy. [L1090.075]

### Contact page

- Older (2021-2022): A contact page should show several ways to reach the business, such as an email address, a phone number, and a physical address, alongside the contact form. This gives customers more than one option to get help. [L1264.015]
- Older (2021-2022): The Contact Us page needs its Template suffix set to 'page.contact' instead of the default 'page' to display a contact form. The form shows fields for Name, Email, Phone Number, Message, and a SEND button. [L1264.013]
- Older (2021-2022): Steps to add the contact form to the Contact Us page: [L1264.014]
  1. Open the Contact Us page under Online Store > Pages.
  2. Scroll to the Template section and open the Template suffix dropdown, which shows 'page' by default.
  3. Select 'page.contact'.
  4. Click Save.
  5. Click View page to check that the contact form appears.

### Mid-page "We are here to help" section

- Older (2021-2022): Build a mid-page help section with this structure: [L1310.032]
  - a heading that says the business is ready to help customers reach their outcome;
  - a line naming the outcome;
  - contact details: phone, email and live chat;
  - one button linking to the contact page.
- Example: [L1310.032]

```
Ready to plan your next move?
[one line naming the goal]
Phone: [phone number] | Email: [email address] | Live chat
[Talk to our team]
```

### Personal video follow-up

- A personal video lets a lead see a face and hear a voice behind the brand. This makes a reply more likely than a generic reminder email. Most competitors do not send such videos, so this method also helps the store stand out. [L1049.009]

## Site speed, mobile and technical hygiene

### Page speed targets

- **Older target, 2021: fully loaded page under 2 seconds.** Look for performance gains when a page loads slower than about 2 to 2.5 seconds. [L0962.047]
- **Older advice, 2021-22: stop at about 5 seconds on average.** Reduce speed work until the store runs at about 5 seconds on average, and move the remaining time to core conversion work. Chasing 1 to 2 second load times usually costs developer time that does not pay back unless a measurable conversion gain shows up. [L1522.043]
- **Both values are given because the sources do not reconcile them.** Use the 2-second figure as the aim for individual pages, and the 5-second average as the point where further speed work needs a measured conversion case. [L0962.047, L1522.043]
- **Slow mobile connections:** Shoppers on slow mobile connections may leave a page that takes more than about 3 seconds to appear, so paid ad clicks are wasted. Example: a visitor from Google Shopping ads looking at a $2,500 dining table left after a 3-second spinner and bought from a competitor. [L1098.003]
- **Judge speed on a weak connection:** Mobile traffic is a large share of ecommerce visits, so mobile load speed matters more than a fast desktop or home Wi-Fi test suggests. Judge store speed by how it performs on a phone with a weak connection, such as an airport network or a slow mobile connection. [L1098.026]
- **Already-good stores:** Website speed scores around GTmetrix grade A with a performance score near 97 are considered very good, and further speed work is not a priority compared with competitors. This is a sanity check for stores already at that level and does not replace the targets above. [L0836.011]
- **Speed and conversion:** Website load time directly affects conversion rate; slower-loading pages result in fewer purchases, making image file optimization critical to ecommerce profitability. (Older advice, 2021-22.) [L0300.001]
- **Speed and bounce:** Product page bounce is reduced by fast load times and clear details. (Older advice, 2021.) [L0801.118]
- **Speed and experience:** Page speed affects how users experience a store and how many of them buy. Page speed optimization should be checked and improved on store pages. (Older advice, 2020.) [L1087.007]
- **Speed and repeat purchases:** Customers who are dissatisfied with page performance are 79% less likely to buy again. Speed affects repeat purchases and therefore the effect of email and retargeting campaigns. [L0390.032]

### Measuring speed

- **Test the page as a customer would (older, 2021-22):**
  1. Load the page on desktop, mobile and tablet.
  2. Load it on different computers, on cellular data and on home Wi-Fi.
  3. On each, check the page speed and look for loading problems. [L0473.016]
- **GTmetrix check (older, 2021):**
  1. Run the URL in GTmetrix.
  2. If the scores show a lot of yellow or red, open the waterfall view.
  3. Find the elements that load furthest to the right.
  4. Remove or optimize the slowest items, such as images or app code. Third-party trackers that load late, such as an email platform script, can be acceptable. [L0962.048]
- **Fastest route to the bottleneck (no year given):** Run the store through GTmetrix or Google PageSpeed to find the main bottleneck, which is usually images or GIFs. [L1259.023]
- **GTmetrix recommendations on Shopify (older, 2021-22):**
  - Recommendations such as leveraging browser caching, reducing DNS lookups, enabling server-side compression and using a CDN are largely controlled by Shopify's hosting, so they can usually be ignored on a Shopify store. Focus on the recommendations about images, scripts, apps and redirects instead. [L1266.042]
  - GTmetrix ranks its recommendations by impact, and the top rules improve the overall score most. Not every rule applies to a given page, so treat the list as generic best practice rather than a checklist to complete. [L1266.043]
